Proposition 21.4 (Deep bad-prime parts). At s = 9 (the 29 parts (e1 , e2 ) with e1 ≤ e2 ≤ 7 at the
split prime 3, plus c = 1), s = 16 (c = 1, p2 ; and 7) and s = 24 (15 parts at 2, up to exponent 8;
and 11), every tested c-part is complete and genus-pure. At s = 18 the 3-parts are pure, but the
coprime part is not, so uL has cusp content. If H̄ = 1 and every Tc is S-invariant, then every
c-part is genus-pure and uL is Eisenstein; conversely, if H̄ 6= 1, the coprime part already gives
cusp content. By Theorem 21.2 every Tc is a union of S-cosets, hence S-invariant, so the coprime
criterion decides.

Status (Category A). Per tested part: exact (matsolvemod lattice solve, completeness of every class,
exact purity test). The reduction lemma, given S-invariance: (1 + j)1Tc is (S + hji)-invariant, hence
H-invariant, and σH = H.


21.1    Local root numbers at s = 7
The 24 cusp characters at s = 7 have conductor of norm 140 (ramified at p2 , p5 , p7 ). The vector-
valued components are Γ(140)-modular and the scalar newforms have level 1402 = 19600; the two
levels are consistent (Category A).

Proposition 21.5 (Local root numbers). For the 24 cusp characters, the local root numbers εv
(p2 , p5 , p7 , ∞) are exact elements of Q(ζ840 ) of modulus 1. εp2 is a 6th root of unity; εp5 is never a
root of unity. The global root numbers have non-monic minimal polynomials (e.g. 5t8 + 6t4 + 5),
so they are not roots of unity. The sector signs are products of local root numbers: Zp = ψp (−1) =
ε(ψp )ε(ψp−1 ) = −1 (p = 5, 7), and Sp2 = ωπp (−1). They are not individual local root numbers.

Status (Category A). The local factors (Tate’s formulas) and the character-side identities are exact.

Lemma 22.10 (q-adic reduction). For any prime q, a quadratic sum y mod qn e((αy 2 +βy+γ)/q n )
reduces by q vq (α) to a unit-leading sum. The unit-leading sum is then evaluated by the odd-q Gauss
sum, or by the explicit 2-adic formula. This gives a closed form for H(c) for every N .

Status (Category A). (i), (ii), (iii), (iv) and Lemma 22.10 have elementary proofs. They were also
checked exactly in Z[ζL ] against brute force: about 7 · 104 , 6.2 · 105 and 7 · 105 tests, with 0 mismatches.
For (v), 144 = lcm(16, 72) is explained by (ii). The periods 16 and 96 are proved conceptually in
Propositions 22.12 and 22.13 (and also by exhaustive exact check across the proved bound 48N ). The
coeﬀicients at x = 14 , 16 are Proposition 22.14.

Proposition 22.11 (Checks of the general formula). The closed form of H(c) given by
the CFP together with Lemma 22.10 agrees exactly with brute force for every N ∈
{2, . . . , 13, 15, 16, 21, 25, 27, 32, 35, 45, 49}, every a coprime to N , every s < 200 and every c. The
runs at N = 32, 35, 45, 49 have 199680, 327600, 421200, 802620 tests and 0 mismatches.
Status (Category A). Exact arithmetic in Z[ζL ], L = lcm(8, 2N ).

23 The cone: origin of ∆, the choice of walls, and Hsaxes
23.1    The lattice origin of ∆ = (s − 2)(7s − 32)
Let A = ( 2s ss ) be the Gram matrix of Q2 (n, k) = n2 + snk + 2s k 2 , so det A = −s(s − 2), and let
c1 = (−1, 1), c2 = (−4, 1) (the choice) and e1 = (1, 0).

Proposition 23.1 (Lattice origin of ∆). (i) 7s − 32 = −2Q(c2 ). Indeed c2 = c1 − 3e1 ,
    B(c1 , e1 ) = s − 2, Q(e1 ) = 1, and −2Q(c2 ) = (s − 2) + 6(s − 2) − 18 = 7s − 32.

 (ii) v := (3s, s − 8) = JAc2 (J the rotation by 90◦ ) spans c⊥
                                                              2 , B(c2 , x) = det(x, v), and u2 = v/g
      with g = gcd(s − 8, 3s) = gcd(24, s − 8).

(iii) 2Q(v) = det A · 2Q(c2 ) = s(s − 2)(7s − 32), so Q2 (u2 ) = s∆/(2g 2 ).

 (iv) For any w with det(w, u2 ) = 1, the residual coeﬀicient in the Gauss phase of Proposi-
      tion 22.9(iii) is det A/(4Q(u2 )) = −g 2 /(2(7s − 32)) = Q(πw), where π is the B-orthogonal
      projection onto Rc2 along u2 .

 (v) ∆ = 4Q(c1 )Q(c2 ) = B(c1 , c2 )2 + det Gram(c1 , c2 ) = 16(s − 2)2 − 9s(s − 2), with B(c1 , c2 ) =
     −4(s − 2) and det(c1 , c2 )2 = 9.

Status (Category A). All identities are symbolic in s. The degenerate prime cases of Proposi-
tion 22.9(iv) are read off: p | 7s − 32 means that c2 is isotropic mod p (the residual sum collapses
to a delta), and p | s − 2 is the c1 analogue. No physical interpretation is offered.

Proposition 23.4 (Status of c2 = (−4, 1)). c2 = (−4, 1) is part of the definition of Hs (§10.4):
it is fixed at s = 7 and kept for all s. Within the class c ∈ c1 + Ze1 (second coordinate b = 1) at
s = 7: Q(ca ) < 0 iff a ∈ {1, . . . , 6}; the closed orthant lies strictly inside the w = 1 region iff a ≥ 4;
and the Cmixed vanishing order is maximal (first nonzero term q 294 ) exactly for the M -class {3, 4}.
Together these give a = 4 uniquely among b = 1. Among all primitive (−a, b) with b ≤ 6 it is not
unique, even up to M : the vanishing order grows as a/b → s/2, and the optimum under the Cmixed
criterion is the axes vector (−7, 2). For s 6= 7 this choice is not forced (the deepest b = 1 class is
a ≈ s/2).

Status (Category A). Exact computations, to q 1200 at s = 7.

Category B. Reading the three conditions as the “selection rule” behind the original choice is an
interpretation.

Remark 23.5 (What is intrinsic in ∆). Combining Propositions 23.1–23.4 (all Category A):



                                                           78
 • the factor s − 2 = −2Q(c1 ) of ∆ is intrinsic given c1 , and c1 is itself forced once one wall is
   the orthant edge n = 0;
                                                       √
 • the factor 7s − 32 = −2Q(c2 ), and with it every ∆ term, every (7s − 32)r denominator
   and every g-dependence in the Hs coeﬀicients of §22, is tied to the choice c2 = (−4, 1); it
   is invariant only under a ↔ s − a (Proposition 23.2), and other admissible c2 give ∆(a) =
   (s − 2)((2a − 1)s − 2a2 ).
These statements concern Hs as defined; they do not change it.

24.2    Modularity status of Cthird
The statements of this subsection are proved or exactly checked, conditional where stated; the
words “quantum modular form” for the factor |cx + d| are terminology (Category B).
Identity. 2Cthird = q 8 f2,7,7 (−q 10 , −q 14 , q) + 1 + E, checked to q 8000 , and
                                                                               
                               Cthird = 12 (1 − θ3 ) + 12 e(7τ /20) ϑhol + EL .
Lemma 24.3 (Parity splitting). At every rational x, along q = e(x)e−t , t → 0+ : e(7τ /20)ϑhol ,
θ3 and q 8 f contain only half-integer powers of t; E = q 7/20 EL contains only integer powers
t−1 , t0 , t1 , . . . . Hence the integer-order radial data of Cthird are those of 21 (1 + E) and the half-
integer ones those of 12 q 8 f .

Status (Category A). Proved, conditional on two-dimensional Euler–Maclaurin (Proposition 13.1)
and Hecke’s continuation theorem.

Theorem 24.4 (Phase obstruction). Let Γ ⊂ SL2 (Z) have finite index, ρ any finite-dimensional
representation, j(γ, τ ) = (cτ + d)k or |cτ + d|k , and V holomorphic with V (γτ ) = j(γ, τ )ρ(γ)V (τ ) +
o(1/y) at every rational x 6= γ −1 ∞. Then Cthird 6= λ · V for every λ; likewise for C, Psorth and E.

Status (Category A). Unconditional. The proof uses G(1/m) = e(7/(20m))P (m mod 140)/m for
gcd(m, 70) = 1 (which also follows from Corollary 14.2(iii)) and a linear-recurrence/essential-singularity
argument.

Theorem 24.5 (Sign obstruction). If V is holomorphic and finite-dimensional on Γ 3 S, T N ,
ρ′ (T N ) is diagonalisable with unimodular eigenvalues, and V (γτ ) = (cτ + d)k ρ′ (γ)V (τ ) + o(1/y),
then none of EL , X = 21 (ϑhol + EL ) or the 72-component E-vector is λ · V . Hence E is not the
holomorphic part of a weight-1 harmonic Maass form, nor a holomorphic weight-1 form, nor mixed
mock with modular factors of weight < 1.
Status (Category A). Conditional on the cited analyticity of harmonic-Maass cocycles, which is
needed only for the class-membership corollaries; the |x| profile law is Theorem 14.1. Objects whose
cocycles are themselves of size ≥ 1/y are not excluded (open).

Class numbers versus genera (Category A for the counts). With Ds the discriminant of the
order, h+ equals the number of genera for every s ∈ {3, . . . , 20, 30} except s = 11 (8 vs 4) and
s = 19 (8 vs 4); also s = 25 (12 vs 4) and s = 41 (24 vs 8).
    The rule “cusp part ⇔ h+ 6= #genera” is false at both levels. For the family span,
dcusp > 0 for every odd s in 5..51, including s = 5, 7, 13, 15 where h+ = #genera (Theorem 20.1).
For uL it fails at s = 14, 17, 18, 22, 23 (Proposition 21.1). Both notions are invisible in the radial
data at x = 0, which are s-uniform (§§25.1–25.2).

25.4   Structural remarks
Remark 25.3.

(a) One improper automorph (Category A).
   The involution ϕ(n, k) = (n, −2n − k − 1) used in the proof of Theorem 25.1 equals −γRs , and
   Rs a = −a. So ϕ, the reflection of Theorem 24.1(ii) and H5 ≡ 0 come from a single symmetry.
   Rs c1 = −c2 holds only at s = 5, which is why H5 ≡ 0 and Q(c1 ) = Q(c2 ) there. Adding Rs to
   hγ, −Ii gives no new label relations for any s = 5, . . . , 25 (exact orbit counts).

(b) The factor 20 (identity Category A; its meaning Category B).
   The denominator of c0 is 4(s − 2)/ gcd(s, 8) = 20, 3, 36 at s = 7, 8, 11, which is the ideal-norm
   factor cs in Na = cs Q. For odd s it equals −B(c1 , c2 ) = −8Q(c1 ) = 4(s − 2) of the Hs cone (at
   s = 8 the two differ: 3 versus 24); equivalently Qs = (Na + s)/(4(s − 2)) for odd s.

(c) Residue and Pell unit (Category A).
                          √              √                        √
   Res = arccosh(s − 1)/ ns = log εs / ns with εs = (s − 1) + ns , the fundamental solution of
   x2 − ns y 2 = 1. For √
                        even s this is the fundamental unit of the order, not of the maximal order:
   at s = 8, ε8 = 7 + 4 3.

(d) ∆ and the s = 8 scale (Category A).
   ∆ = (s − 2)(7s − 32) = 4Q(c1 )Q(c2 ) = 16(s − 2)2 − 9ns ; B(c1 , c2 )2 − 4Q(c√   1 )Q(c2 ) = 9ns ;
                                                                                               √
   det(c1 , c2 ) = 3; gcd(7s−32, s−8) = g and gcd(7s−32, s−2) = gcd(18, s−2). Q( ∆) 6= Q( ns )
   for every s < 106 (exact search), so the Hs half-integer data live in a different quadratic field

   from the E-residue except where ∆ is a square. At s = 8 all the listed half-integer coeﬀicients
         √
   lie in π Q (Theorem 22.1(v)).

(e) Three parities are one (algebra Category A; same-ρ identification Category B).
   The label parity P = Slab  2 , the cone-weight parity ν 7→ −ν and the integer versus half-

   integer radial orders coincide. A weight-k vector-valued form satisfies ρ(S)2 f = (−1)k f ,
   so the E/Eisenstein sector lies in WP and the Zwegers/ϑhol sector in Fix(−P ) (dimension
   68 = 4(3 · 3 + 2 · 4) at s = 7). Hence weight-1 vectors cannot realise any part of V ⊥ ⊂ WP .
   Category B: that Zwegers’ completed theta transforms under this same 140-dimensional ρ with
   the Slab = ρ(S)−1 sign.

(f) The constants aH , aC are closed forms (Category A).
    aH = H−1/2 (7) and aC = C−1/2 (7) symbolically; the t−1/2 coeﬀicient of Cthird at x = 0,

(iii) dimFℓ Hom(ρN2 , (Slab , Tlab )) = 4, and a random element has rank 72: the two normalisations
      agree only on the 72-dimensional subspace WP .

Status (Category A). Exact computation modulo `. The isomorphism itself is Theorem 26.2. By
semicontinuity the characteristic-0 Hom dimensions are at most 8 and 4. Category B (reading): the
value 8 is consistent with eight pairwise non-isomorphic irreducible sectors W5± ⊗ W7± ⊗ {A3 , B1 } (§19);
that the characteristic-0 dimension equals 8 is not separately proved here.

Remark 26.4 (Dual, twist, metaplectic sign). ρlab ∼         6= ρ∗N2 (Theorem 26.2(iii)); no twist by a
character of SL2 (Z) is needed; sign(N2 ) = 0, so there is no metaplectic sign. The Weil representation
depends only on the discriminant form, so any even lattice with discriminant form ∼         = qN2 and
signature ≡ 0 mod 8 (e.g. N2 ⊕ U , N2 ⊕ E8 , and the lattice T of Proposition 26.5, up to dualisation)
gives the same representation.

26.3    The transcendental lattice
Let ΛK3 = U 3 ⊕ E8 (−1)2 (even unimodular, signature (3, 19)), and let U ⊕ U ⊂ ΛK3 have bases
e1 , f1 , e2 , f2 with ei · fi = 1 and all other products 0.
Proposition 26.5 (Explicit primitive embedding and T ). Put n1 = e1 +f1 (norm 2), n2 = e2 −35f2
(norm −70), t1 = e1 − f1 (norm −2), t2 = e2 + 35f2 (norm 70).
  (i) span(n1 , n2 ) ∼
                     = N2 and is primitive in U ⊕ U (the 2 × 2 minors of (n1 n2 ) have gcd 1).
 (ii) t1 , t2 are orthogonal to n1 , n2 and span the full orthogonal complement of span(n1 , n2 ) in
      U ⊕ U (index 1; their Gram determinant is −140).

(iii) Hence the orthogonal complement of N2 in ΛK3 is

          T := N2⊥ = h−2i ⊕ h70i ⊕ U ⊕ E8 (−1)2 ,           sign(T ) = (2, 18),      AT ∼
                                                                                        = Z/2 × Z/70,
                           2   y 2
       with qT (x, y) = − x4 + 140 = −qN2 (x, y) under the identity identification of generators. The
       gluing is the one induced by the unimodular overlattice U ⊕ U (consistent with Nikulin [13],
       Prop. 1.6.1).
Proof. Direct computation: n1 · n1 = 2, n2 · n2 = −70, n1 · n2 = 0, t1 · t1 = −2, t2 · t2 = 70, and
ni · tj = 0. The complement of a rank-2 primitive sublattice of the unimodular U ⊕ U has rank 2
and | det | = 140; span(t1 , t2 ) lies in it with | det | = 140, so the index is 1. The other summands
U ⊕ E8 (−1)2 are orthogonal to U ⊕ U . The discriminant form of h−2i ⊕ h70i is read off as in
Proposition 26.1.

Status (Category A). Elementary; exact. Nikulin’s existence theorem is not needed, since the em-
bedding is explicit.

Corollary 26.6 (The residual law in K3 terms). ρlab ∼
                                                    = ρN 2 ∼
                                                           = ρ∗T = ρT (Borcherds’ convention;
honest SL2 (Z)-representations).

Proof. sign(T ) = −16 ≡ 0 mod 8 and qT = −qN2 (hence bT = −bN2 ) under the identification
of Proposition 26.5, so in the formulas for ρT (S) and ρT (T ) every phase is the conjugate of the
corresponding phase for ρN2 . Combine with Theorem 26.2.

Status (Category A). Purely lattice-theoretic; no cited geometric theorem is used. In words: the
140-label residual law transforms under the dual Weil representation of a signature-(2, 18) lattice, which
(Theorem 26.7) is the transcendental lattice of an N2 -polarised K3 surface. This is a statement about
a shared representation; it is not a statement that any residual series is a K3 generating function (see
Remark 26.11).


26.4    Geometric realisation
Theorem 26.7 (N2 -polarised K3 surfaces). (i) The primitive embedding N2 ,→ ΛK3 is unique
    up to O(ΛK3 ); hence T is unique up to isometry.

                                                    = N2 and TX ∼
 (ii) There is a projective K3 surface X with NS(X) ∼           = T.

(iii) N2 represents neither −2 nor 0 (non-trivially). Hence such an X contains no smooth rational
      curves and admits no elliptic fibration, and its ample cone is the whole component of the
      positive cone containing an ample class.

Proof sketch. (i) Nikulin [13, Thm. 1.14.4] (p. 126 of the translation): ΛK3 is even unimodular
of signature (3, 19), N2 is even of signature (1, 1), 3 > 1, 19 > 1, and rk ΛK3 − rk N2 = 20 ≥
2 + l(AN2 ) = 4. (ii) Morrison [12, Cor. 2.9(i)] (p. 112; proved there via Cor. 1.9): for ρ ≤ 10
every even lattice of signature (1, ρ − 1) is the Néron–Severi lattice of some algebraic K3 surface    
(equivalently, surjectivity of the period map); with (i), TX ∼ = T . (iii) x · x = 2 (x1 + 7y)2 − 35y 2 ;
−2 would need u2 − 35y 2 = −1, impossible since −1 is not a square mod 7; 0 would need 35 to
be a square. Without (−2)-classes the ample cone is a full component of the positive cone, and an
elliptic fibration requires a non-zero isotropic class in NS (standard, [9]).

Status (Category A). Category A (cited): we use Nikulin [13, Thm. 1.14.4] and Morrison [12,
Cor. 2.9(i)], and standard facts on ample cones and elliptic fibrations [9]. The arithmetic in (iii) is exact.

Proof sketch. By Nikulin’s gluing criterion [13], Cor. 1.5.2, an isometry gNS ⊕gT of N2 ⊕T extends to
H 2 (X, Z) iff ḡNS and ḡT agree under the glue isomorphism AN2 ∼ = AT . By Proposition 26.8, γ̄ 2 = +1
and r̄ = −1, compatible with idT and −idT , which are Hodge isometries. Both preserve the positive
cone, hence (Theorem 26.7(iii)) the ample cone. By the strong Torelli theorem (Piatetski-Shapiro–
Shafarevich, Burns–Rapoport; e.g. [9], Thm. 7.5.3) they are induced by unique automorphisms

ι; ι acts by −1 on H 2,0 , so it is non-symplectic. The eigenvalues of f ∗ are (6 ± 35)2 on NS ⊗ R
and 1 on T , and the entropy is the logarithm of the spectral radius (Gromov–Yomdin [6]). For
(iii): every automorphism acts on TX by ±1, so its restriction to N2 preserves the positive cone
and acts on AN2 by ±1; by Proposition 26.8(iii) these restrictions are exactly γ 2j and rγ 2j , which
form hγ 2 i ⋊ hri ∼= D∞ ; the action on H 2 is injective by Torelli. Since γ̄ 6= ±1, γ does not lift.

Proposition 26.10 (Lifting γ). (i) If NS(X) ∼
                                            = N2 , then the Hodge isometries of TX are ±1,
    and γ does not lift to Aut(X).

 (ii) If γ lifts on some X with N2 ⊂ NS(X) primitive and M = N2⊥ ⊂ NS(X), then M carries the
      2- and 7-parts of AN2 with rk M ≡ 4 (mod 8), or the 2- and 5-parts with rk M ≡ 6 (mod 8).
      Hence ρ(X) ≥ 6.




                                                     88
                                                
(iii) Let M = A1 (−1)2 ⊕ K7 with K7 = −2   1 −4 . There is an even rank-6 gluing N2 ⊕ M ⊂ L
                                              1

      and an isometry gM of M such that γ ⊕ gM ⊕ idT is a Hodge isometry of H 2 (X, Z) for
      every K3 surface X with NS(X) ∼  = L (such X exist by [12, Cor. 2.9(i)]). On this rank-6
      Noether–Lefschetz locus some w ◦ (γ ⊕ gM ), w ∈ W (L), is induced by an automorphism, so
      Aut grows there.
Proof sketch. (i) The reduction γ̄ ∈ O(AN2 ) has order 2: it swaps on the 2-part, is +1 on the 5-part
and −1 on the 7-part, so γ̄ 6= ±1. The Hodge isometries of TX form a cyclic group µm , with TX ⊗ Q
a Q(ζm )-vector space. If m > 2, a prime p | m gives, after passing to the kernel of the action on
AT , an element of order p without fixed vectors acting trivially on AT . This forces pAT = 0, which
is impossible because AT ∼ = Z/2 × Z/70 is not p-elementary. So every automorphism acts on TX
by ±1, hence on AN2 by ±1, and γ cannot lift. (ii) The glue must carry γ̄ to an isometry of AM .
Milgram’s formula, with Gauss-sum signatures 4, 2, 4, 6, 6 mod 8 for the relevant p-parts, gives the
two congruences. (iii) An exact lattice computation gives the anti-isometric glue and gM (the swap
on A1 (−1)2 and −1 on K7 ) with ḡM ◦ φ = φ ◦ γ̄. Because L contains (−2)-vectors, γ ⊕ gM ⊕ id
need not preserve the ample chamber. The strong Torelli theorem [9, Thm. 7.5.3] supplies a Weyl
element w for which w ◦ (γ ⊕ gM ⊕ id) is induced by an automorphism.

Status (Category A). (i), (ii): written proofs. (iii): exact lattice computation, together with [12,
Cor. 2.9(i)] and the strong Torelli theorem (cited), so it is conditional on these citations. Open: whether
w = 1 can be taken, i.e. whether γ itself lifts on this locus.

Remark 26.11 (Category B and open items for this section). None of the following is a result of
this paper.
 (a) Noether–Lefschetz / Heegner series (Category B). The Noether–Lefschetz/Heegner generating
     series of the N2 -polarised family are vector-valued modular forms of weight 1 + 18/2 = 10 for
     ρT or ρ∗T (depending on convention; Borcherds, Kudla–Millson, Maulik–Pandharipande, cited
     by name only). The residual objects of Theorems 11.6–11.7 are weight-0 Maass-type objects
     with λ = 41 . A shared representation (Corollary 26.6) is not a shared form; no theta-lift or
     Borcherds-product link has been checked.
 (b) Special members (open). Whether γ itself lifts on the rank-6 locus of Proposition 26.10(iii),
     or on any X with ρ(X) ≥ 6.
 (c) Interpretation (Category B). Any physical or “unification” reading of X, f or ι.
 (d) Scope. Every K3 statement in this paper concerns N2 , because the 140-label representation
     is ρN2 and not ρN1 (Theorem 26.2(iii)).

27.2   Three levels of uniqueness
We separate three levels:

 (κ) functions on Q (radial profiles and constant terms);

 (u) λ = 14 Maass vectors and their positive-frequency q-series;

 (Φ) real-analytic weight-1 period functions on R \ {0}, i.e. the Lewis–Zagier class [10].

In this subsection ρ is the representation in which Corollary 14.2 and Theorem 11.6 are written.

Proposition 27.3 (The κ-level gauge). Let G be the space of functions g : Q → C140 with g(γx) =
|cx + d| ρ(γ)g(x) for all γ ∈ SL2 (Z) and all x ∈ Q with γx ∈ Q. Then dim G = dim Fix(T ) = 1.
A basis is g0 (h/m) := m2 G(h/m) (gcd(h, m) = 1, √     m > 0), where G is the profile vector of
Corollary 14.2. Moreover kg0 (h/m)k = m kg0 (0)k = m 70.

Proof. Dimension. SL2 (Z) acts transitively on P1 (Q). So g is determined by g(0), and g(0) must
be fixed by the stabiliser of 0, which is ±ShT iS −1 . On this stabiliser |c · 0 + d| = 1. Hence
g(0) ∈ ρ(S) Fix(T ) ∩ Fix(P ) = ρ(S)Cv0 (Proposition 27.2(ii)), so dim G ≤ 1. Equality holds
because g0 6= 0 lies in G. Equivariance of g0 . Corollary 14.2(i) gives G(γx) = |cx + d|−1 ρ(γ)G(x).
If x = h/m in lowest terms, the denominator of γx is m′ = m|cx + d|, and m′2 = m2 |cx + d|2
turns weight −1 into weight +1. Norm. Choose γ with γ(h/m) = 0; then |c h/m + d| = 1/m.
        P14.2(i) gives G(0) = m ρ(γ)G(h/m), and unitarity gives kG(h/m)k = kG(0)k/m. Finally
Corollary
G(0) = α e(α,0) has kG(0)k2 = 70.

Status (Category A). Proved, given Theorem 14.1 and Corollary 14.2. Consequence: the only ambi-
guity at level κ is the radial gauge Cg0 , which grows like m along h/m. Two κ-level objects with the same
law whose difference is o(m) along rationals coincide, and the canonical radial normalisation q = e(x)e−t
removes the gauge. The constant coboundary Φv0 (x) = v0 − |x|Slab v0 (the analogue of Lewis–Zagier’s
1 − z −2s ) solves the homogeneous Φ-level law. It is excluded once the q 0 term is prescribed.

to 10−11 ; and ST −1 S −1 g0 (0) = g0 (0) to 4 · 10−15 .

Corollary 27.6 (Cocycle and period function determine u). For d ∈ M (ρ): if cd ≡ 0 on H, then
d = 0. Moreover, the restriction Φ|(0,∞) of the period function of Theorem 11.7 determines u, and
hence the positive-frequency series f .

Status (Category A). Proved below; rests on Theorem 27.4.

Proof (outline). Key step. Let e ∈ M (ρ) and suppose that on the first quadrant ce (z) = r(z) with
r rational. The S-relation of Theorem 27.4(b) with ς(z) = +1 gives Ie (z) = z −1 Slab Ie (−1/z) + r(z)
there. Ie is holomorphic on H, z 7→ −1/z preserves H, and z −1 is holomorphic there, so this relation
continues analytically to all of H. Applying it twice, with Ie (−1/z) = −z Slab Ie (z) + r(−1/z), gives
Ie = −P Ie + (rational). Since P e = e, P Ie = Ie , so 2Ie is rational. fe+ decays exponentially as
Im z → ∞, so fe+ = 0, and e = 0 by Theorem 27.4(b). First claim. If cd ≡ 0, apply the key step
with r = 0. Second claim. Φ (in the normalisation of §15) and cu differ by an explicit constant-
coboundary term, rational in z with coeﬀicient au , and a scalar. (0, ∞) lies in the right half-plane,
where cu is holomorphic (and Ω = Ψ, Theorem 11.7(i)). Suppose u, u′ ∈ M (ρ) have the same Φ
on (0, ∞). By the identity theorem, cu−u′ is rational on the first quadrant, and the key step gives
u = u′ .

Theorem 27.7 (Uniqueness and residual ambiguity of the vector completion). Let u = ulift , with
positive-frequency series f and period function Φ (Theorems 11.6, 11.7).

  (i) Uniqueness given strong data. Among d ∈ M (ρ), u is the only element with positive-frequency
      series f and q 0 -coeﬀicient au . Moreover, among period functions of elements of M (ρ), Φ is
      determined by its restriction to (0, ∞).

 (ii) Non-uniqueness under weak conditions. Consider period functions of elements of M (ρ) with
      the same ρ-law (weight 1, factor |cx + d|), the same singular I/t data, and real-analytic on
      R \ {0}. They form the aﬀine space Φ + {Φc : c ∈ S(ρ)}, enlarged by C Φv0 when the q 0 term
      is not prescribed. The parametrisation c 7→ Φc is injective.

(iii) The cusp space is nonzero. dim S(ρ) ≥ 1. An explicit witness is ucusp := ΠB1 X − ulift , the
      projection to the ρ-stable sector B1 X − . It lies in M (ρ), has zero constant term because
      Fix(T ) ∩ B1 X − = 0, and is nonzero because its coeﬀicient at n = 14019
                                                                               is nonzero. Hence, by
      the injectivity in (ii), the class in (ii) contains more than one element.

approximate eigenbasis Q; these choices are not part of the proof. The margin is at least 1012 in every
sector.

Remark 27.16 (Further remarks on §27). (a) Period functions of cusp forms. We use Deitmar–
   Hilgert [5, Thm. 3.3 and Thm. 4.4] with Γ = PSL2 (Z), the finite-image representation η =
   ρ and spectral parameter ν = 0 (our λ = 41 , as ∆u = ( 14 − ν 2 )u; the hypotheses ν ∈          /
                                                                       −1
   2 + Z and Re ν > − 2 hold). The dictionary is ψ(x) = ρ(S) Φ(x)/x on x > 0, with
   1                       1

   Φ(x) = κ(−1/x) − |x|Slab κ(x); ψ satisfies the three-term equation η(T )ψ(z) = ψ(z + 1) +
   (z + 1)−1 η(ST −1 )ψ(z/(z + 1)), and the growth conditions of [5, Thm. 4.4] read Φ(x) = o(1)
   as x → 0+ and Φ(x) = o(x) as x → ∞. Under these conditions period functions correspond
   bijectively to elements of S(ρ), so by Theorem 27.13 they form the line spanned by Φucusp .
   Lewis–Zagier [10] treats the scalar case for SL2 (Z) only. The non-cusp form ulift is not covered
   by either reference. Status: Category A (cited; hypotheses checked against [5]); the numerical
   check of the dictionary (residual 7 · 10−15 ) is Category B.

28 Obstructions to coeﬀicient-emitting maps for gs
One may ask whether the coeﬀicients of gs (§25.2), or the cone data, can be produced (“emitted”) by
a natural construction from E8 data or from the integer pattern PW(7, 11; 35 ): B ′ = 72 · 11 = 539,
window 18, fibres 68 = 3 · 11 + 5 · 7 and 67, towers 243 = 35 , body 521, auxiliary 8 (the rank of
E8 ). We prove obstructions for several natural classes of such maps (Propositions 28.1–28.4) and

                                                      97
record what is not excluded (Remark 28.5). Data of this kind can select the parameter s; once s is
fixed, gs is defined independently of E8 and of PW, so selecting s does not emit coeﬀicients.

Proposition 28.1 (The t1 obstruction for modular-type sums). Let s > 0 with s 6= 3.

  (i) gs (e−t ) = 2 + 2(s − 3) t + (5s2 − 44s + 75) t2 + O(t3 ) as t → 0+ (Theorem 25.2). For
      s = 1, 7, 8, 11: (c1 , c2 ) = (−4, 36), (8, 12), (10, 43), (16, 196).

 (ii) Let F be any finite sum of holomorphic modular or quasimodular forms of nonnegative real
      weights
      P         (any level, any multiplier) that are holomorphic at the cusp 0. Then F (e−t ) =
               −a with all a ≥ 0, up to an exponentially small error. Hence g is not such a sum.
        a Ca t                                                               s

(iii) Nor is any single shift q r gs (r ∈ R) such a sum; equivalently, gs 6= q −r F . Cancelling the
      t-term forces r = s − 3, and the t2 coeﬀicient of q s−3 gs is then 2(2s2 − 19s + 33), which is
      nonzero for s = 7, 8, 11 (values −4, 18, 132) and for s = 1 (value 32).

The class in (ii) contains θE8 = E4 , every θE8 (q m ), the Eisenstein series built from σ3 and their
derivatives, and every lattice theta series, of E8 or of any other positive-definite lattice.

Proof. (i) is Theorem 25.2 (c0 , c1 , c2 were also re-derived exactly). (ii) For a form of weight k,
F (e−t ) is (2π/t)k times the expansion of F |k S at i∞, i.e. C t−k plus exponentially small terms. A
quasimodular form of depth p ≤ k/2 contributes t−k+j with 0 ≤ j ≤ p, so all exponents are ≤ 0.
Finitely many such terms give only t−a with a ≥ 0, whereas gs has the coeﬀicient c1 = 2(s − 3) 6= 0
of t1 . (iii) With q = e−t , q r gs = e−rt gs (e−t ) has t-coeﬀicient 2(s − 3) − 2r and, at r = s − 3,
t2 -coeﬀicient c2 − rc1 + r2 = 2(2s2 − 19s + 33).

Status (Category A). Proved, given Theorem 25.2. Category B support: (gs −c0 −c1 t−c2 t2 )/t3 at t =
10−3 , . . . , 1.25 · 10−4 is −1355, −1338, −1329, −1325 for s = 7. This is consistent with the value c3 (7) =
−3962/3 ≈ −1320.7 of Theorem 25.2, and similarly for s = 1, 8, 11 (c3 = −546.7, −1566.3, −303.3).
Sanity: the same test excludes f0 = g1 , consistent with f0 being mock.

Proposition 28.2 (E8 is invisible to the vector and cone data). E8 is even unimodular, so AE8 = 0
and ρL⊕E8 = ρL for every even lattice L. Consequently the Weil representation of Theorem 26.2,
the cone/vector transformation law, the eight sectors of Proposition 27.2, the Hom dimension 8
(Proposition 26.3) and the q-exponents modulo 1 of the 140-vector are unchanged by adjoining E8 .
No construction that factors through discriminant-form data can carry E8 information into them.

Status (Category A). Standard (discriminant forms of orthogonal sums are orthogonal sums). Arith-
metic aside, not a result: the primes involved are {2, 5, 7} at s = 7, {2, 3} at s = 8 and {2, 3, 11} at
s = 11. Only 7 and 11 are also E8 exponents.

Proposition 28.3 (Exact linear-span test to q 1499 ). Let F be the following 1036 distinct integer
sequences, truncated to q 0 , . . . , q 1499 :

 F1 q r θE8 (q m ) for 1 ≤ m ≤ 12 and 0 ≤ r ≤ 20 (252 series);

 F2 W (E8 ) dominant-weight counts per norm, rescaled by 1 ≤ m ≤ 6 and shifted by 0 ≤ r ≤ 20
    (126 series);
                             Q
 F3 the W (E8 ) Molien series d∈{2,8,12,14,18,20,24,30} (1 − q d )−1 , with the same rescalings and shifts
    (126 series);


                                                       98
  F4 partition counts into parts from each nonempty subset of {7, 8, 11, 18, 67, 68, 243, 521, 539}
     (511 series);

   δ δn,r for 0 ≤ r ≤ 20 (21 series). No two of the 1036 series coincide.

None of g7 , g8 , g11 , g1 , g7 − g8 , g11 − g7 lies in the Q-span of F. Moreover g1 , g7 , g8 , g11 are
Q-linearly independent.

Proof. For p = 261 −1, the mod-p rank of F is 880. The exact Q-rank of F4 ∪ δ is 376 (532 columns;
exact integer rank). F1–F3 have 504 columns, so rankQ F ≤ 504 + 376 = 880. For an integer matrix
rankp ≤ rankQ , so rankQ F = 880. Adjoining any one target raises the mod-p rank to 881, so its
Q-rank is at least 881 > 880, and the target is not in the Q-span of the truncated sequences, a
fortiori not of the full ones. The independence statement is an exact rank-4 computation.

Status (Category A). Exact. Sanity checks of F2: d(1..4) = 1, 1, 1, 2 and rE8 (4) = 17520 = 17280 +
240.

Proposition 28.4 (No low-degree rational generating function). For s ∈ {7, 8, 11} and (R, n0 ) ∈
{(1000, 0), (1000, 999), (600, 1200)}, the (R + 1) × (R + 1) Hankel matrix (an0 +i+j )0≤i,j≤R of the
coeﬀicients of gs is nonsingular modulo p = 261 − 1, hence over Q. Consequently gs 6= P/Q with
P, Q ∈ Q[q], deg Q ≤ 1000 and deg P ≤ 999. The same holds with deg Q ≤ 600 and deg P ≤ 1200.
                                                P                 P
Proof. Suppose gs = P/Q with 0 6= Q =                     k
                                                  k≤D bk q . Then
                                                                                   n
                                                                    k bk an−k = [q ]P = 0 for all
n > deg P . Let R ≥ D and put P     bk = 0 for D < k ≤ R and cj = bR−j (0 ≤ j ≤ R). Row i of the
Hankel matrix applied to c gives k bk an0 +i+R−k , which vanishes when n0 + R > deg P ; all indices
are ≥ n0 ≥ 0. So the matrix is singular whenever D ≤ R and deg P ≤ n0 + R − 1. Nonsingularity
at (R, n0 ) = (1000, 0) therefore excludes deg Q ≤ 1000, deg P ≤ 999, and at (600, 1200) it excludes
deg Q ≤ 600, deg P ≤ 1799, which contains the stated range.

Status (Category A). Exact modular arithmetic. This excludes, for example, any finite partition-
count or Molien-type map with total part sum ≤ 1000, and any linear combination of such maps with
common denominator of degree ≤ 1000. The W (E8 ) Molien degree sum is 128.

Remark 28.5 (Scope: what is not excluded). Propositions 28.1–28.4 exclude only the stated
classes:

  • modular and quasimodular sums of nonnegative weight holomorphic at 0, with at most one
    q r shift;

  • constructions factoring through discriminant-form data;

  • Q-linear combinations of the 1036 tested sequences;

  • rational generating functions in the stated degree ranges.

They do not exclude:

  • nonlinear or non-modular maps;

  • mock or false-theta constructions with E8 input;

  • sums with several different q r prefactors;


                                                  99
  • weakly holomorphic forms with poles at the cusp 0;
  • rational functions of higher degree;
  • linear combinations of untested sequences.
Category B (reading): absence of a map in the tested classes is evidence, not proof, that no natural
map exists.


29 Consequences
29.1   No scalar residual law; the law that exists
Proposition 29.1 (No scalar residual law). (i) There is no identity C = F + E with F an alge-
    braic combination of holomorphic modular forms, signature-(1, 1) mock (Zwegers) holomorphic
    parts, unary, partial or false theta series, and E(e−t ) = o(1/t).
 (ii) There is no quotient law C · g = F with holomorphic modular g 6≡ 0, F .
(iii) There is no scalar W7 law: for no weight k and no constant λ is C(τ ) − λ(7τ )−k C(−1/(7τ ))
      radially bounded at all rationals.

Status (Category A). This restates Theorem 11.5 and §13.5: (i), (ii) are conditional on Baker’s
theorem and the q-expansion principle, using the leading term C(e−t ) = I/t + aC t−1/2 + · · · and aC 6= 0;
(iii) is an exact finite computation: at 178 of the 270 test rationals (denominator < 30), including
x = 5/7, exactly one of G(x), G(−1/(7x)) vanishes (Theorem 11.4).

Proposition 29.2 (The law that exists has weight 1 and is vector-valued). (i) Where          both
    Gauss means are nonzero, |G| obeys an exact weight-1 relation with |λ| = 7−1/2 (§13.5); the
    leading 1/t radial profiles obey the S/T law with factor 1/|x| (Theorem 14.1); and the vector
    cocycle has automorphy factor |cx + d| (Theorem 11.7).
 (ii) The vector law consists of the 140-label Siegel lift to λ = 14 Maass forms (Theorem 11.6),
      the Lewis–Zagier period formula (Theorem 11.7), the 42-dimensional function span V =
      VEis (36) ⊕ Vcusp (6) (Theorem 11.8), the Herglotz closed form (Theorems 11.9, 11.10), the
      identification ρlab ∼
                          = ρN2 (Theorem 26.2) and the uniqueness statements of §27. It is a law
      for the vector of cone sums, not for scalar C.

(iii) The characteristic a = A−1 (0, 2s )T = 2(s−2)

Remark 29.3 (Weight 12 cannot carry the residue). Any weight- 12 harmonic Maass form has radial
growth O(t−1/2 ), so no weight- 12 object can carry the I/t term of C (Category A, conditional on
the standard, cited t−1/2 radial bound).
Remark 29.4 (Sample values). Four values 19.80, 1.82, 9.24, 20.90 at x = 1/7, 3/7, 5/7, 1
that have been proposed as “residual samples” are not radial values of C: |C(0.995 e(x))| ≈
19.5, 18.5, 19.9, 54.0 (floating point, Category B). The meaningful radial targets are
limt→0 t C(e(x)e−t ) = I · G(x) at every rational x (Theorem 11.4; Category A).

                                                    100
29.2   Uniformity in s
Proposition 29.5 (What depends on s = 7). 7s − 32 = −2Q(c2 ) for the wall c2 = (−4, 1)
(Proposition 23.1); only the factor s − 2 of ∆ is intrinsic given c1 (Remark 23.5). The results are
otherwise uniform in s: the tensor theorem holds for all odd s (Theorem 19.5), the radial closed
forms are rational functions of s, and Theorem 25.2 holds for all real s > 0. The structurally
special values are s = 5 (the radial coeﬀicients of §22 vanish; H5 ≡ 0) and s = 8 (the cone of Hs
is the axes cone, ∆ = 144 = 122 ).

Status (Category A). As proved in §§22–23.


29.3   The case s = 1
g1 = f0 , the fifth-order mock theta function (Hickerson–Mortenson [8]). At s = 1 the lattice ( 21 11 )
haspdet = −s(s−2) = 1 > 0 and is positive definite, so the indefinite part of the construction (Hs , C,
Q( s(s − 2))) does not specialise; Hs is Zwegers-admissible only for integer s ≥ 5. Theorem 25.2
gives a proved radial expansion of gs uniform in s, including s = 1. Quantum modularity of gs is
not claimed.

29.4   A formal contact with A5
PSL2 (F5 ) ∼
           = A5 . At s = 7 the label representation has the tensor factor ρ2x2 /5 (Theorem 19.3);
VEis = A3 ⊗ W5+ ⊗ W7+ contains W5+ (dimension 3, factoring projectively through A5 ) and Vcusp =
B1 ⊗ W5− ⊗ W7− contains W5− (dimension 2, a faithful representation of SL2 (F5 ) = 2 · A5 ). This is
standard theory together with Theorem 19.3. Numerically (Category B) the S, T images on W5±
generate groups of order 120 with scalar subgroup {±1} and quotient of order 60. Any further
reading of this contact is Category B.

29.5   Status of three questions
For reference we record the status of three questions about C: (N1) a map from E8 or PW data
that emits the coeﬀicients; (N2) a unique completed period; (N3) a tail. (These labels are unrelated
to the lattices N1 , N2 of §26.)

    mark 25.3(e)); whether an exotic single kernel produces both sectors of Cthird .

    (dim V (578) ≤ 83532); composite m and g-consistency for all odd m in Proposition 20.4.

33 The 35-component law vector
Notation is that of §2. In Part III, x denotes a rational number h/m with m ≥ 1, gcd(h, m) = 1
(and m = 1 for x = 0).

33.1   Gauss means
Definition 33.1. For α, β ∈ Q2 and x ∈ Q the function ν 7→ e(xQ(ν) + B(ν, β)) on α + Z2 is
invariant under ν 7→ ν+M u (u ∈ Z2 ) for every positive integer M divisible by a suitable M0 (α, β, x),
because xQ(ν + M u) + B(ν + M u, β) − xQ(ν) − B(ν, β) = xM B(ν, u) + xM 2 Q(u) + M B(u, β) and
B(ν, u), Q(u), B(u, β) have bounded denominators. The Gauss mean is
                                          X                                  
                      Gα,β (x) = M −2           e xQ(α + n) + B(α + n, β) ,
                                        n∈Z2 /M Z2

which does not depend on the admissible M .

   Let hA = ( 21 , 0). For µ ∈ D we use the representative of µ in [0, 1)2 .

Definition 33.2 (The 35-component law vector). For µ ∈ D put

                  K1µ = Ga+µ,a ,     K2µ = Ga+µ,0 ,        K3µ = Gµ,a ,   K4µ = Gµ,0 .

The vector (K2µ )µ∈D is the 35-component law vector; K3 and K4 are its S- and T -companions.
V35 denotes the complex span of the 105 functions K2µ , K3µ , K4µ on Q.

Lemma 33.3 (Elementary rules). For α, β ∈ Q2 and x ∈ Q:

  (i) Gα+n,β = Gα,β for n ∈ Z2 ;

 (ii) Gα,β+λ = e(B(α, λ)) Gα,β whenever Aλ ∈ Z2 ;

(iii) Ggα,gβ = Gα,β for g ∈ GL2 (Z) with g T Ag = A.

Proof. (i) α + n + Z2 = α + Z2 . (ii) For ν = α + n, B(ν, λ) = B(α, λ) + nT Aλ ∈ B(α, λ) + Z.
(iii) ν 7→ gν maps α + Z2 bijectively onto gα + Z2 , commutes with translation by M Z2 up to the
bijection g of Z2 /M Z2 , and Q(gν) = Q(ν), B(gν, gβ) = B(ν, β).

ce_exact/orbits.py). Lower bound. At the 216 rationals X216 = {h/m : 1 ≤ m ≤ 15, 1 ≤
h ≤ 3m, gcd(h, m) = 1} every value Gα,β (h/m) is a finite mean over a 4m × 4m box and lies in
R = Z[ζL ][1/(4m)2 ] with L = 3 531 528 000. For a prime p ≡ 1 (mod L), sending ζL to a primitive
L-th root of unity in Fp defines a ring homomorphism R → Fp , under which the rank of a matrix
cannot increase. The 216 × 105 value matrix has rank 36 modulo each of p = 17 657 640 001,
21 189 168 001, 35 315 280 001, 42 378 336 001 (A51_bridgeC_exact/rank216.py). So the rank in
characteristic 0 is at least 36.

Proposition 33.7 (Exact SL2 -relations and irreducibility; computer-assisted). Let MS , MT be the
matrices of S and T on V35 in the basis of orbit representatives.
                                                                √        P             k
 (i) MS4 = I and (MS MT )3 = MS2 exactly over Q(ζ9800 ), with 35 = −i k mod 35 35   k
                                                                                       ζ35 .

 (ii) dim EndSL2 (Z) (V35 ) = 1, and V35 is isomorphic to the 36-dimensional summand E36 of the
      decomposition E36 ⊕ C6 of the 42-dimensional lift representation of Part II at s = 7; E36 and
      C6 are irreducible.
In particular every non-zero vector of V35 generates V35 under S and T .
Proof. (i) Exact reduction modulo the cyclotomic polynomial Φ9800 of all 1296 entries (A51_brid
geC_exact/sl2_exact.py). (ii) The intertwining equations were solved over Fp at two primes;
a dimension over Fp bounds the dimension in characteristic 0 from above, and the identity gives
dim End ≥ 1. An explicit isomorphism X : V35 → E36 was verified exactly modulo Φ58800 (X SV =
SE X in all 1296 entries, X a product of invertible matrices; A35_lawspace_exact/exact_intertw
iner.py). The lift representation is unitary, hence semisimple, and its commutant has dimension
2 (exact upper bound, A32_35_to_rho7/exact_hom.py); with two non-zero invariant summands
E36 , C6 this forces both to be irreducible and non-isomorphic. The final statement holds for any
irreducible representation.

34.2    Decomposition into pieces
A piece is a triple (c, U, σ) with c ∈ Z2 , U = (u1 u2 ) ∈ M2 (Z) and a sign σ; it stands for the sum
over c + U Z2≥0 . A ray is a pair (c, u) with weight σ, standing for the sum over c + uN.

Lemma 34.4 (Periodicity). Let x = h/m, N = 2m, and ϕ(ν) = e(xQ(ν)+B(ν, β)) for ν ∈ α+Z2 ,
with (α, β) one of the 105 pairs or (α, β) = (a, 0). Then ϕ(ν + N u) = ϕ(ν) for all u ∈ Z2 .
Consequently, for every piece, f (z) := ϕ(α + c + U z) is N -periodic in each coordinate, and for every
ray b 7→ ϕ(α + c + bu) is N -periodic.

Proof. ϕ(ν + N u)/ϕ(ν) = e(xN B(ν, u) + xN 2 Q(u) + N B(u, β)). Here Aν ∈ Aα + Z2 , and Aα ∈ Z2
(if α = µ) or Aα ∈ (0, 27 ) + Z2 (if α = a + µ), since Aµ ∈ Z2 . So B(ν, u) = uT Aν ∈ 21 Z and
xN B(ν, u) ∈ hZ. Q(u) ∈ 21 Z gives xN 2 Q(u) ∈ 2mhZ. B(u, 0) = 0 and B(u, a) = 27 u2 give
N B(u, β) ∈ mZ.

36 Order four: the shadow candidate and the law at order t0
The next two questions are (a) whether A1 becomes S-covariant after subtracting an explicit shadow
contribution, and (b) whether the t0 coeﬀicient A2 of Theorem 35.6, or A2 minus a completion term,
satisfies a weight-0 vector-valued law.
    Both questions were tested numerically; neither produced a Category A statement, and the
results are recorded only in the following remarks and in Appendix D.
Remark 36.1 (Category B: the sawtooth shadow candidate). The candidate for (a), modelled
on anPerf-completion, removes all sawtooth groups: the completed coeﬀicient is A1 − Ξε with
Ξε = λ ελ W [λ], ε = (1, 1, 1), that is A1 −Ξε = D +R (Proposition 35.11). Its S-image is not in its
span, so this candidate does not close; nor does any of the six secondary subsets ε 6= (0, 0, 0), (1, 1, 1).
The sign control (closure of A0 at weight 1, failure at the wrong weight) passed (item D.8). The
candidate is not the actual completion: the erf-completion constants of H7 have not been derived
(Problem 1.).
Remark 36.2 (Category B: a weight-0 law for A2 ). For (b), A2 does not satisfy a
pure weight-0 vector-valued law, nor one of weight 21 or 1; a mixed law A2 (−1/x) ∈
span[A2 (x), |x|1/2 A1 (x), |x|A0 (x)] is not closed either, but this test has little discriminating power
(rank 214 on 216 points). The sign control passed (item D.9).


37 Shadow structure of the companion series
Throughout this section Q(ν) = ν12 + 7ν1 ν2 + 72 ν22 , B(µ, ν) = Q(µ + ν) − Q(µ) − Q(ν), so that

companion pairs (αj , βj ) of Definition 34.1 (families K2, K3, K4). For a function f on R2 we write

Lemma 37.3 (parity of a weight-one law). Let F : H → C105 and χ ∈ C× with F (−1/τ ) =
χ τ 35−1/2 M F (τ ) for all τ ∈ H. Then F = −χ2 P F .

Proof. Apply the law at −1/τ : F (τ ) = χ(−1/τ )35−1/2 M F (−1/τ ) = −χ2 35−1 M 2 F (τ ) =
−χ2 P F (τ ) by Proposition 37.2.

Lemma 37.4 (no even weight-one law). Let F 6≡ 0 satisfy the law of Lemma 37.3 and in addition
F (τ + 1) = T F (τ ). Then χ = 1 and P F = −F .

Proof. The hypotheses read F |1 S = χ35−1/2 M F and F |1 T0 = T F . Hence F |1 (ST0 ) =
(χ35−1/2 M F )|1 T0 = χ35−1/2 M T F and, iterating, F |1 (ST0 )3 = χ3 35−3/2 (M T )3 F = χ3 P F by
Proposition 37.2. Since (ST0 )3 = −I and F |1 (−I) = −F , we get χ3 P F = −F . Lemma 37.3 gives
χ2 P F = −F . Subtracting, χ2 (χ − 1)P F = 0; as P is invertible and F 6≡ 0, χ = 1, and then
P F = −F .

Corollary 37.5. If F satisfies the hypotheses of Lemma 37.4 for some χ, then its P -even part 12 (F +
P F ) vanishes. No nonzero P -even vector satisfies a weight-one law with matrices (35−1/2 χM, T )
for any χ.

Lemma 37.9. Let A > 0, δ, c ∈ R. Then ϑ(τ ) =                   m∈δ+Z m e(Am τ + cm) ≡ 0 on H if and only
                                                                            2

if 2δ ∈ Z, 2c ∈ Z and 2cδ ∈ Z.

                                            2
2δ ∈ Z. If 2δ ∈ / Z, the coeﬀicient of q Am for any m 6= 0 is m e(cm) 6= 0. If 2δ ∈ Z, the coeﬀicient
       2
of q Am (m > 0, m ∈ δ + Z) is m(e(cm) − e(−cm)) = 2im sin(2πcm), which vanishes for all such m

37.6    Line terms of the literal series
Write ν = α + v with α the representative of Definition 34.1 and v = (n, k) ∈ Z2 . The literal weight
is W (v) = 1[n ≥ 0]1[k ≥ 0] − 21 (sgn n + sgn(n + 21k)). Put σ = α1 + 21α2 , m = n + 21k and

h1 = 1[n ≥ 0] − 12 (1 + s1 ),   h2 = 1[k ≥ 0] − 12 (1 + s2 ),   g1 = sgn n − s1 ,   g3 = sgn m − sgn(m + σ).

Proposition 37.12. W (v) − p(ν) = 12 (1 + s1 )h2 + 12 (1 + s2 )h1 + h1 h2 − 12 g1 − 21 g3 . Each of h1 , g1
(resp. h2 ; g3 ) is supported on the integers n (resp. k; m) in the closed interval between 0 and −α1
(resp. −α2 ; −σ).

Proof. By definition 1[n ≥ 0] = 21 (1 + s1 ) + h1 and 1[k ≥ 0] = 12 (1 + s2 ) + h2 ; multiplying,
1[n ≥ 0]1[k ≥ 0] = 14 (1 + s1 )(1 + s2 ) + 21 (1 + s1 )h2 + 12 (1 + s2 )h1 + h1 h2 . Also sgn n = s1 + g1
and sgn(n + 21k) = s3 + g3 since ν1 = n + α1 and ν1 + 21ν2 = m + σ. Subtract p. The functions
n 7→ 1[n ≥ 0] and n 7→ 12 (1 + sgn(n + α1 )) agree unless n and n + α1 lie on different sides of 0 or
one of them is 0, which confines n to the stated interval; likewise for the others.

    Hence S W − S p is a finite sum of unary series, by type:

 (a) half-lines ν2 = c fixed (weightPh2 · 12 (1 + s1 )): Q = w2 − 35  2           7
                                                                   4 c , w = ν1 + 2 c, a partial theta in

37.7    Summary
Theorem 37.13. For the 105 companions:

 (i) the P -odd part of S p equals the holomorphic part of 21 G     b O − H,
                                                                          b whose ξ1 -image is 1 (X c′ −
                                                                                               4
     X c1 ) − 21 X c2 and involves exactly three distinct weight- 23 unary thetas;

(ii) the P -even part admits no weight-one law with the matrices (χ35−1/2 M, T ) (Corollary 37.5);

(iii) the orthant Siegel lift Φorth has the Laplacian defect of Theorem 37.11, a single product of the
      weight- 32 unary series ϑ+ (in 72 m2 ) and the conjugate of ϑ− (in 25 u2 ), vanishing iff ρm or ρu
      preserves the companion;

(iv) the literal series differs from S p by the line terms of Proposition 37.12.

Remark (Category B) 37.14. The identification of the holomorphic projection of Φorth with S E
and the independent tests of the P -even law and of the vanishing set are numerical; see Appendix D,
items D.10, D.12, D.13.

Remark 37.15. Descriptively, the P -odd part is a local (cone-type) mock modular object of weight
one and the P -even part is a non-local (arc-integral) object whose defect lives on the endpoint ray;
these words describe the structure above and are not further statements.

39.4    Improper automorphs and the orbit of the corner
Proposition 39.12 is stated for g ∈ GL2 (Z) with g T Ag = A and its proof does not use det g; it
therefore applies verbatim to improper automorphs, with Γg defined by the same reduction rules.
From the defining relation, Γg1 g2 = Γg2 Γg1 .

Lemma 39.18. For every unimodular cone C = R≥0 u1 + R≥0 u2 , Cor−C = CorC .

and Q(−ui ) = Q(ui ). Since B̄1 is odd and vanishes on Z and B2 is even, each summand of the
corner formula is unchanged, with the same phases.

          RY (1/x) = −x−1 Π (kM )−1 RY (x),         hence      kRY (1/x)k = |x|−1 kRY (x)k.

40 Local modular constituents
The modular foundation developed in Parts I–III is completed on the local side by a short chain of
identities for smooth representations of GL2 over p-adic and finite fields, together with a filtration-
weight determination for a Serre–Katz theta power. The results of this section are stated in the
language of Breuil, Breuil–Mézard, and Colmez; they do not modify the radial or S-law theorems
already proved for the Bridge Series C, and they do not resolve Problem 1..
    Throughout, fix a prime p ≥ 3. Write G = GL2 (Qp ), B = P for the upper-triangular Borel,
T for its diagonal torus, and K = GL2 (Zp) (or, more generally, GL2 (OF ) for an unramified finite
extension F/Qp ). Let K1 = ker K ↠ Γ be the first congruence subgroup, with Γ ∼          = GL2 (Fp ).
Smooth representations are understood over Fp unless stated otherwise. The seven Category A
theorems of this section are promoted from already-captured proofs; two partial count statements
are recorded without Category A completeness status.

40.1    Compact and ordinary induction
                                                                                      ×
Theorem 40.1 (Equality of compact and ordinary induction). Let χ : P → Fp be a smooth
character inflated through T . Then

                                           c-IndG        G
                                                B χ = IndB χ.

Explicitly,                        
                       IndG
                          Bχ =         f : G → Fp smooth : f (bg) = χ(b)f (g) ,
with G acting by right translation.

Proof. Write G0 = P = B(Qp ), closed in G, and let R = χ be the one-dimensional smooth
representation of P inflated through T . Compact induction consists of smooth functions f : G → R
with f (g ′ g) = g ′ · f (g) for g ′ ∈ G0 whose support is compact modulo G0 , with G acting by right
translation. Ordinary induction is the same function space without the compact-support condition.
By the Iwasawa decomposition G = P K, the quotient P \ G is compact, so the compact-mod-P
support condition is automatic. Hence the two functors agree on characters inflated through T .

                                                   127
Status (Category A). Proved. Compact induction equals ordinary induction for characters inflated
through T , by Iwasawa compactness of P \ G.


40.2    Socle via the first congruence subgroup
Theorem 40.2 (Socle reduction to K1 -invariants). Let π be any smooth representation of K (or
of G) over Fp . Every irreducible smooth K-subrepresentation of π lies in π K1 , and
                                                                
                               socK π = socK π K1 = socΓ π K1 .

In particular, for a principal series π = πα ,
                                                             
                                       socK (πα ) = socΓ παK1 .

Proof. The subgroup K1 is an open normal pro-p subgroup of K, the kernel of K ↠ Γ. Let
σ ⊂ π be an irreducible smooth K-subrepresentation over Fp . Smoothness and irreducibility for
profinite K imply that σ is finite-dimensional. The image of K1 in GL(σ) is a finite p-group
acting on an Fp -space, so σ K1 6= 0. Since K1 is normal, σ K1 is K-stable; irreducibility forces
σ K1 = σ, hence σ ⊂ π K1 . Therefore every irreducible K-subrepresentation of π lies in π K1 , and
socK π = socK (π K1 ). The group K acts on π K1 through Γ, so K-subrepresentations of π K1 are
precisely Γ-subrepresentations, and socK (π K1 ) = socΓ (π K1 ).

Status (Category A). Proved. Socle of any smooth Fp -representation of K reduces to the Γ-socle of
the K1 -invariants.

                                      B(1, ω) = IndB ω ⊗ ω −1 = πα .

40.4   Filtration weight of a Serre–Katz theta power
Let f be the modular form of level Γ0 (7) labelled 7.4.a.a in the ambient mod-7 setting, and write
w = w(θ3 f ) for the filtration weight of its Serre–Katz theta cube. Odd weights vanish on Γ0 (7) with
trivial character. The convention is the minimal even k such that θ3 f mod 7 lies in Mk (Γ0 (7)) mod
7.
Proposition 40.4 (Filtration bounds). One has

                        w(θ3 f ) ≤ 10,      w(θ3 f ) 6= 2,     w(θ3 f ) 6= 4.

Consequently the only remaining even possibilities in the standard range are w ∈ {6, 8, 10}.
Proof. Rebuild f from E4 , E4 (7z) and g 2 with g = 7E2 (7z) − E2 (z), checking Hecke relations
a4 = a22 − 8 and a6 = a2 a3 . Compare the mod-7 q-expansion of θ3 f against spans in weights 2, 4
and 10 built from E4 , E6 , their 7z-rescalings, g and f , through 120 coeﬀicients. In weight 2 the
span has rank 1 = dim M2 (Γ0 (7)) and the augmentation by θ3 f has rank 2, so w 6= 2. In weight 4
the ranks are 3 and 4, so w 6= 4. In weight 10 the span rank equals the augmented rank, and the
Sturm bound 10 · 8/12 < 7  120 upgrades the coeﬀicient match to an identity of forms, proving
w ≤ 10. Odd weights are zero as noted.

Status (Category A). Proved. Filtration bounds w(θ3 f ) ≤ 10 and w 6= 2, 4, by span ranks and the
Sturm bound in weight 10.

Theorem 40.5 (Filtration weight ten).

                                          w(θ3 f ) ∈
                                                   / {6, 8}.

Combined with Proposition 40.4,
                                           w(θ3 f ) = 10.
Proof. The dimensions are dim Mk (Γ0 (7)) = 1 + 2bk/3c for even k ≥ 2, so dim M6 = dim M8 = 5.
Enlarge the weight-6 and weight-8 spans by integral generators: weight-3 η-quotients with character
(·/ − 7), namely h = (η(z)η(7z))3 , u = η(z)7 /η(7z), v = η(7z)7 /η(z), their pairwise products, and
g 3 ; weight 8 is obtained by multiplying the weight-6 set by g and the weight-4 set by g 2 . Reduced
mod 7 on 120 coeﬀicients, both spans attain rank 5 = dim Mk . By the q-expansion principle the
reduction fills Mk (Γ0 (7), Z) ⊗ F7 . Augmenting by θ3 f raises the rank to 6, so θ3 f lies outside
M6 and M8 mod 7. Thus w ∈       / {6, 8}. With Proposition 40.4 and the vanishing of odd weights,
      3
w(θ f ) = 10.

Status (Category A). Proved. w(θ3 f ) = 10, by full-span ranks in weights 6 and 8 together with the
bounds of Proposition 40.4.


40.5   Socle of Breuil’s principal series
Theorem 40.6 (Breuil socle formula). Let ρ be absolutely irreducible with
                                                p(r+1) 
                               ρI ∼
                                  = ω2r+1 ⊕ ω2           ⊗ ωm,

so that π ∼                  KZ Sym /T ) ⊗ χ ◦ det in Breuil’s notation. Then, for every p ≥ 3
          = π(r, 0, χ) = (indG      r

(including p = 3),
                                                           r     m
                           socK π ∼
                                  = Symr ⊕ Symp−1−r ⊗ det ⊗ det .

                                                 129
Proof. By Breuil’s correspondence, π ∼   = π(r, 0, χ). Twisting reduces to m = 0. The space π I(1) has
dimension 2. Every irreducible Fp [K]-representation has one-dimensional I(1)-invariants, and every
nonzero smooth K-representation has nonzero I(1)-invariants, so socK π has at most two irreducible
summands. The map v 7→ [Id, v] : Symr → π is nonzero, hence injective. The isomorphism π ∼          =
π(p − 1 − r, 0, ω r ) likewise embeds Symp−1−r ⊗ detr into π. These two images are non-isomorphic: if
r 6= p−1−r the dimensions differ; if r = (p−1)/2 then detr is nontrivial and the I(1)-eigencharacters
differ. Their sum is therefore direct inside the socle, and the dimension bound forces equality.

Status (Category A). Proved for all p ≥ 3, including p = 3. Socle of Breuil’s π(r, 0, χ) is (Symr ⊕
Symp−1−r ⊗ detr ) ⊗ detm .


40.6    Irreducible representations of the finite general linear group
Proposition 40.7 (Shape of irreducible K-representations). Every irreducible smooth Fp [K]-
representation, K = GL2 (Zp ), is of the form
                                                               a
                                           σ ∼
                                                        2
                                             = Syms Fp ⊗ det

inflated from GL2 (Fp ), for some 0 ≤ s ≤ p − 1 and a mod (p − 1), and

                                             dim σ I(1) = 1,

where I(1) denotes the pro-p Iwahori subgroup of K.
Proof. Let K(1) = ker(K → GL2 (Fp )), an open normal pro-p subgroup. For a nonzero smooth
representation V in characteristic p, V K(1) 6= 0; if V is irreducible then V = V K(1) , so V factors
through GL2 (Fp ). On Syms with the standard action, U (Fp ) fixes the line Fp xs . Any nonzero
subrepresentation W has W U 6= 0, hence contains xs ; conjugating by the antidiagonal Weyl element
and expanding ut · y s with invertible Vandermonde for s ≤ p − 1 fills all monomials, so each
Syms ⊗ deta is irreducible. For the invariants: I(1) surjects onto U (Fp ), and writing g(Y ) = f (1, Y )
the condition ut f = f forces g constant for deg g ≤ p − 1, hence dim σ I(1) = 1.

Status (Category A). Proved. Irreducible smooth K-representations are Syms ⊗ deta inflated from
GL2 (Fp ), with one-dimensional I(1)-invariants.

Proposition 40.8 (Count of p-regular classes). The number of p-regular conjugacy classes in
GL2 (Fp ) equals p(p − 1), matching the label count of the family
                                      a
                               Syms ⊗ det : 0 ≤ s ≤ p − 1, a mod (p − 1) .

Proof. p-regular elements are the semisimple ones. Their classes are: p − 1 scalars; (p − 1)(p − 2)/2
split non-scalar diagonal classes; and (p2 − p)/2 anisotropic classes. The sum is p(p − 1).

Remark 40.9. Proposition 40.8 is recorded for continuity. Completeness via Brauer’s theorem is
not invoked; Theorem 40.10 supplies a Brauer-free classification. This count alone is not promoted
to Category A as a completeness statement.
Theorem 40.10 (Classification of irreducibles). Every irreducible Fp -representation V of GL2 (Fp )
is isomorphic to Syms ⊗ deta for some 0 ≤ s ≤ p − 1 and a mod (p − 1). The argument uses nonzero
U -coinvariants and induction from the Borel, without Brauer’s theorem. For p ∈ {3, 5, 7}, every
pair (s, a) occurs as a socle constituent of some IndG
                                                     B ψ.


                                                  130
Proof. Let B = T U be the upper Borel. Since U is a p-group in characteristic p, (V ∨ )U 6= 0,
so VU 6= 0. The torus T acts semisimply on VU ; choose a character ψ through which a nonzero
quotient factors. Frobenius reciprocity yields an injection V ,→ IndG      B ψ. Each Sym ⊗ det has
                                                                                            s     a

one-dimensional U -coinvariants, and as (s, a) varies every character of T arises, so some W on the
Sym-list embeds into the same principal series. The space (Indψ)U is two-dimensional. If ψ 6= ψ w ,
the socle analysis on the two weight lines shows every irreducible submodule is isomorphic to a
Sym-twisted representation. If ψ = ψ w , then Indψ splits as constants plus Steinberg, both on
the Sym-list after twisting by a power of det. Thus every irreducible V is of the stated form. For
p ∈ {3, 5, 7} a finite check confirms that every (s, a) appears as a socle constituent of some principal
series.

Status (Category A). Proved, Brauer-free. Every irreducible of GL2 (Fp ) is Syms ⊗ deta ; verified for
p ∈ {3, 5, 7} that every label occurs in a socle.

41 Local cite-lifts and residual packaging advancements
This section records three Category A cite-lifts from the residual path-problem (RPP) group-ring
calculus—named Local cite-lifts F1–F3 to distinguish them from the unrelated E8 filter families F1–
F3 of Section 28—together with the November 2026 packaging advancements that fix the ambient
Hilbert newspace, the thin form complex, and the static cell budget. The Local cite-lifts are
independent of the local modular constituents of Section 40. Nothing in this section settles the
residual question for the Bridge Series C, identifies the shadow Ξ, or produces an explicit formula
for a boundary vector of C inside V35 .
    Throughout, fix an integer M ≥ 2 and write G = Z/M Z. Let Z[G]      P be the group ring, and let
ρ : Z[G] → MatM (Z) be the regular representation, so that for h = g ag [g] the matrix ρ(h) is the
                                       P                                           Q
circulant with eigenvalues ĥ(k) = g ag ω kg , ω = e2πi/M , and det ρ(h) = k ĥ(k). Given a step
alphabet C ⊂ Z, weights w = (w1 , . . . , wn ) and integer multiplicities ai,c , set
                     X
               hi :=    ai,c [c · wi ] ∈ Z[G],    T := hn · · · h1 ,  |T | := |hn | · · · |h1 |,
                     c∈C
             P
where |h| = g |ag |[g]. For a source residue rs and a target residue rt , write W = W (rs → rt ) for
the signed path weight (ρ(T ))rt ,rs and N = N (rs → rt ) for the unsigned path weight (ρ(|T |))rt ,rs .
Write k · k1 for the `1 -norm of coeﬀicients and spr for the spectral radius over C.

41.1   Local cite-lift F1: weak NP-completeness on an odd-M slice
Theorem 41.1 (Local cite-lift F1). Consider the residual path problem (RPP) of deciding whether
there exists a path of signed weight summing to a prescribed target residue modulo M . Restrict to
the slice in which M is part of the input, C = {0, 1}, every multiplicity equals 1, and
                                                           X
                                                           n
                                      M = 2k + 1 > Σ :=           wi
                                                            i=1


                                                  131
for some k ≥ 1. Then:
  (i) for every odd M , one has 1 + ω j 6= 0 for all j, hence det ρ(hi ) 6= 0 for each layer and
      det ρ(T ) 6= 0;
 (ii) RPP restricted to this slice is weakly NP-complete (membership via a polynomial certificate;
      hardness by reduction from Subset Sum through the odd modulus M = 2k + 1 > Σ).
The same decision problem is false for fixed odd M ≤ Σ: the instance M = 3, w = {1, 2}, target 0
has no nonempty subset summing to 0 over Z, yet {1, 2} ≡ 0 (mod 3). Completeness is weak only:
a pseudo-polynomial dynamic program of cost O(nM ) applies.
Proof. (i) If 1 + ω j = 0 then ω j = −1, so (−1)M = ω jM = 1, Q     contradicting M odd. Hence each
ĥi (k) = 1 + ω kw i (or 2 when wi ≡ 0) is nonzero, and det ρ(T ) = i det ρ(hi ) 6= 0. (ii) The standard
Subset-Sum reduction with modulus 2k produces layers with det ρ(hi ) = 0 whenever some wi is odd
(evaluate at frequency M/2). Replacing the modulus by the odd integer M = 2k + 1 > Σ restores
nonvanishing of every determinant while preserving the subset-sum encoding of yes/no instances,
because the encoding uses only the inequality M > Σ (and, for no-instances, a reachable-residue
argument independent of parity). Membership is the usual short certificate that a path realises the
target; checking that the instance lies on the slice is polynomial for C = {0, 1} and unit coeﬀicients.
The counterexample at fixed M = 3, w = {1, 2}, target 0 is immediate. Weakness follows from the
standard O(nM ) DP on residues.

Status (Category A). Cite-lift. Weak NP-completeness of RPP on the odd-M = 2k + 1 > Σ unit-
coeﬀicient slice with C = {0, 1}; false for fixed odd M ≤ Σ. Distinct from the E8 filter family F1 of
Section 28.


41.2   Local cite-lift F2: nonvanishing determinant does not prevent cancellation
Proposition 41.2 (Local cite-lift F2). Nonvanishing of det ρ(hi ) does not preclude signed path
cancellation. Explicitly, for M = 3,
                              h1 = [0] + [1],       h2 = −[0] + [1] + 3[2],
one has det ρ(h1 ) = 2 and det ρ(h2 ) = 36, yet at target residue 1 the two contributing paths cancel:
W = 0 and N = 2.
                                                                                   Q
Proof. The eigenvalues
                 Q2      of a circulant are the DFT evaluations, so det ρ(h1 ) = 2k=0 (1 + ω k ) = 2
and det ρ(h2 ) = k=0 (−1 + ω k + 3ω 2k ) = 36. The two length-2 paths from residue 0 to residue 1
have signed weights 1 · (−1) and 1 · 1, summing to W = 0, while the unsigned count is N = 2.

Status (Category A). Cite-lift. Explicit M = 3 counterexample: det ρ 6= 0 coexists with W = 0,
N = 2. Distinct from the E8 filter family F2 of Section 28.

Remark 41.4 (Scope of the Local cite-lifts). The three Local cite-lifts F1–F3 exhaust the Cate-
gory A content drawn from the group-ring reading of the residual path problem. They are combi-
natorial and scale-free. No normalisation scalar enters them, and they do not identify any modular
form, any shadow series, or any boundary vector of C.

41.6    Classical arithmetic corrections
Proposition 41.9 (Classical exponents and conductors). The following classical facts are recorded
for reference; none is promoted as a bound for the Bridge Series C or for the Local cite-lifts.
  (i) The classical Burgess exponent on character sums is 3/16, not 1/5.
 (ii) The conductor of the elliptic curve Y 2 = X 3 −35X is 78400, not 560. This curve is a different
      object from V35
                   Hilb .


(iii) The Kim–Sarnak bound θ = 7/64 applies to Maass forms; for holomorphic cusp forms of
      weight 2 one has θ = 0 by Deligne.
No Burgess bound, no Kim–Sarnak bound, and no conductor-560 statement is used as a hypothesis
anywhere in this paper.
Proof. (i) is Burgess’s theorem. (ii) is the Cremona/LMFDB conductor of the curve with Weier-
strass model y 2 = x3 − 35x (minimal discriminant and conductor 78400 = 26 · 52 · 72 ). (iii) is
Deligne’s theorem for holomorphic weight 2, contrasted with the Kim–Sarnak hybrid bound for
Maass forms.

Status (Category A). Recorded classical facts only: Burgess 3/16; conductor 78400; Kim–Sarnak
7/64 not for holomorphic weight 2.


                                                  134
41.7    The thin form complex and the rank-one classifier D1
Let X× = K9 × PB ′ be the product of the complete 9-vertex charge skeleton with the residual
path of length B ′ = 539, and let F be the thin residual form complex on the locked generators
{1, α, ω2 , µ, f, δf } with product differential D, under the residual relations ω2 = dQ α and µ =
−B ′ ω2 on ordered triples, truncated by F 3 := 0 (brutal truncation σ≤2 ). Write sαδ for the mixed
generator α ⊗ δf .
Theorem 41.10 (Rank-one classifier D1 ). In the thin truncated complex F one has

                      H 0 (F ) ∼
                               = Q,       H 1 (F ) = 0,      H 2 (F ) ∼
                                                                      = Q · [sαδ ],

and consequently the formality equivalence F ≃ Q[0]⊕Q[−2]. Define D1 to be the Q-linear functional
on H 2 (F ) that isolates the line Q · [sαδ ]. Then:
  (i) D1 is a static rank-1 classifier over Q, spanned by [sαδ ] ∈ H 2 (F );

 (ii) D1 acts only on the nine charge sectors of X× and takes no argument from any temporal
      variable, execution schedule, or metric normalisation;

(iii) no algebraic datum of D1 is shared with any resonant counter set;

 (iv) Layer 2 structure beyond the definition of D1 is not constructed from D1 in this paper.
The class [sαδ ] is the generator of coker(D1 : F 1 → F 2 ) in the thin truncation; in the filled cochain
complex of X× the same cochain is not a cocycle whenever δf 6≡ 0.
Proof. The cohomology computation H 0 (F ) ∼    = Q, H 1 (F ) = 0, H 2 (F ) ∼
                                                                            = Q · [sαδ ] is the locked
computation on the thin residual form complex: Df = vδ forces f ∈ F 0 , the relation ker D =
span{vδ } = im(D : F 0 → F 1 ) kills H 1 , and after quotienting by the exact pure-charge pieces
the mixed class [sαδ ] spans H 2 . Formality F ≃ Q[0] ⊕ Q[−2] is the unique formal model with
these Betti numbers. Items (i)–(iii) are the definition of D1 as the projection onto that line, with
domain restricted to the static charge sectors and with no structure map into any resonant counter.
Item (iv) records that no further Layer 2 construction is derived from D1 here. The filled-complex
caveat is the identity Dsαδ = −δf on prisms, which is nonzero on the residual data.

Status (Category A). Rank-1 over Q, spanned by [sαδ ] ∈ H 2 (F ) ∼
                                                                 = Q; H 1 (F ) = 0; formality F ≃
Q[0] ⊕ Q[−2]; Layer 2 not constructed from D1 .


41.8    Static cell budget and Residual versus Resonant
Proposition 41.11 (Static cell budget Seﬀ ). Let T3 denote the ternary map of Proposition 42.6 and
write 539 for the residual cardinality of Proposition 42.6. Define the effective packaging structure
Seﬀ by the following constraints.
  (i) The integer T3 = 539 is a static cell capacity (equivalently: a static arithmetic hash budget).
      It is not a stopping time, not a convergence rate, and not a runtime execution length forced
      by residual cohomology.

 (ii) There is no global guarantee that a packaging remainder Ω satisfies Ω ≡ 0 (mod 539).

(iii) Dual-rail representations, when mentioned, are hardware-only side-channel countermeasures
      and play no algebraic role in Seﬀ .

                                                  135
 (iv) The assignment                                                         
                                 M : Seﬀ −→ Residual539 × Resonant539
       keeps the two factors disjoint: the residual factor is a count of leftover cells, the resonant
       factor is a forced round count; a count is not a clock.

Proof. Items (i)–(iv) are definitional constraints on the packaging arithmetic already present in
Proposition 42.6 and Remark 42.7: the identity 539 = b4859/9c = 18+521 records a cell count and,
separately, a window-plus-body split used as a schedule; neither identity produces a congruence
Ω ≡ 0 (mod 539) nor a structure map from residual cohomology into the schedule. Dual-rail
hardware is excluded from the algebraic criteria by (iii).

Status (Category A). T3 = 539 is a static cell/hash budget, not a stopping time; no global Ω ≡ 0
(mod 539); dual-rail hardware-only; Residual 6= Resonant.

Proposition 41.12 (Residual is not Resonant). The residual cardinality 539 (a count of leftover
cells per charge sector after packaging) is a different mathematical object from the resonant schedule
length 539 (a forced iteration count of T3 ). Equality of the two integers is an equality of values, not
a structure map of chain complexes, graded vector spaces, or sets. In particular, residual product
cohomology does not force the resonant schedule, and the resonant schedule does not redefine residual
cohomology.

Proof. By construction of the residual product complex, B ′ = 539 is the number of residual path
edges in each sector. By construction of the resonant schedule, the same integer is used as a
constant-time iteration budget. No chain map, graded map, or set map relating C • (X× ), F , or
H • (F ) to the counter set {1, . . . , 539} is part of either definition (Theorem 41.10(iii) and Proposi-
tion 41.11(iv)).

Status (Category A). Residual cardinality 6= resonant schedule; equality of integers is not a structure
map.


41.9    Status of claims not established

and are not used as hypotheses in this paper.

  (i) A Petersson inner-product formula for the shadow Ξ is not used as a proved identity.

 (ii) No Kronecker 11-element purge and no four-forces (or SU (3) continuum) mapping is part of
      the theory.

(iii) The assertion C ∈/ V35
                          Hilb still lacks an explicit formula for a boundary vector of C; the elliptic

      curve Y 2 = X 3 − 35X is not a substitute.

Proof. (i)–(iii) are negative inventory statements: no written Petersson identity for Ξ is cited as
proved; continuum and four-forces maps are excluded from every Category A statement above; and
without an explicit formula for a boundary component of C the membership C ∈     / V35
                                                                                    Hilb cannot be

evaluated.

Status (Category A). Status only: Ξ Petersson not used as proved; Kronecker/four-forces maps
excluded; C ∈
            / V35
               Hilb
                    lacks an explicit formula for C.
