Part IV
Foundation
42 The foundation attempt: what ties RTA to the framework
This section lists the written RTA objects and, for each, what is proved about its relation to
the structures of Parts I–III. References to “RTA” are to the RTA manuscript (Residual Ternary
Algebra: Packaging Identities, Residual Series gs , and Bridge Series C, draft of 24 August 2026),
which has no algebraic axioms: its six numbered rules are process rules.

42.1   Proved links
Proposition 42.1 (Identification of objects). The following hold.

  (i) The RTA residual series gs is the series gk of Part I with k = s.

 (ii) The RTA double-sum identity for (−q s ; q s )∞ gs holds for every s ≥ 1 as an identity in Z[[q]]
      (Proposition 2.2).

(iii) The RTA path quadratic Qs , cones c1 , c2 , weight w, cone series Hs , orthant series and Bridge
      Series C = P7orth − H7 are those of §2 and of Part II; Caxis and Cthird are those of Part II,
      §10; the RTA natural characteristic χnat (7) = ( 107
                                                           , − 51 ) is the vector a.

Proof. (i) and (iii) are comparisons of definitions: RTA Definition 6.1 and §§9–10 against Defi-
nition 2.1, §2 and Part II, §10; χnat (s) = (s/(2(s − 2)), −1/(s − 2)) at s = 7 equals a. (ii) is
Proposition 2.2.

Corollary 42.2 (Residue law of g7 and its converse). g7 (d) = 0 whenever d ≡ 3, 5, 6 (mod 7).
Conversely, g7 (d) 6= 0 for every d ≡ 0, 1, 2, 4 (mod 7) outside the finite set Z7 of 24 elements listed
in Appendix B (max Z7 = 247), and the sign of g7 (d) is (−1)d+1 for all such d ≥ D0 (7).

Proof. By Proposition 3.2 (Part I), g7 (d) is a sum over n ≥ 0 with n2 ≡ d (mod 7); the squares
modulo 7 are 0, 1, 2, 4, so the sum is empty for d ≡ 3, 5, 6. The converse is the case k = 7 of
Theorem 8.1 (Part I, §8.1), parts 1 and 2.

Theorem 42.3 (The normalized Bridge Series and the 35-component law vector). The following
hold.

  (i) The leading radial profile of the RTA Bridge Series C is I e(7x/20)K20 (x), and it does not
      lie in V35 .

 (ii) The leading radial profile of q −7/20 C is I K20 , a non-zero element of V35 ; together with its S-
      and T -images it spans V35 , and it obeys the vector-valued S-law of Theorem 34.7.

                                                  137
(iii) Up to order t0 , the radial expansion of q −7/20 C at every rational point is given in closed form
                                                                    √
      by Theorem 35.6; at order t−1/2 the coeﬀicient divided by π is algebraic, and at every point
      of Z0 ∪ {− 15 } it is non-zero.

Proof. Theorems 34.5, 34.6, 34.7, 35.6, Corollary 35.8 and Proposition 35.10.

42.2   Negative results
Proposition 42.4 (No scalar residual law). There is no scalar modular, mock-modular (signature
(1, 1)) or unary/partial/false theta law for C of the kinds listed in Theorem 11.5; in particular for
no weight k and constant λ is C(τ ) − λ(7τ )−k C(−1/(7τ )) radially bounded at all rationals.

Proof. Theorem 11.5, with the stated dependence on Baker’s theorem for part (i).

Proposition 42.5 (No vector-valued Fricke law at leading order; computer-assisted). Let D7 f (x) =
f (7x). Then V35 ∩ D7 V35 = 0 as spaces of functions on Q. In particular D7 V35 6⊂ V35 .

Proof. Both spaces have dimension at most 36 (Proposition 33.6). The 216×210 matrix of values of
the 105 functions and of their dilations by 7 at X216 has rank 72 modulo each of the four primes of
Proposition 33.6 (A53_RTA_maps); by the reduction argument of that proof its rank in characteristic
0 is at least 72. Hence dim(V35 + D7 V35 ) = 72 and the sum is direct.

Proposition 42.6 (Facts about T3 and 539). Let T3 (3k) = k, T3 (3k + 1) = 4k + 2, T3 (3k + 2) =
2k + 1.

  (i) If 539 = a1 + · · · + a8 with positive integers and max ai − min ai ≤ 1, then {ai } =
      {68, 68, 68, 67, 67, 67, 67, 67}.

 (ii) For M coprime to 6 and L ≥ 1, as n runs over Z/3L M , T3L (n) mod M is well defined and
      takes each value exactly 3L times.

(iii) Extended to Z3 , T3 preserves Haar measure, and the itinerary map n 7→ (T3j (n) mod 3)j≥0 is
      a homeomorphism Z3 → {0, 1, 2}N conjugating T3 to the shift.

Proof. (i) Let q = min ai and let b be the number of parts equal to q + 1. If b = 8 then 8 | 539, false.
So 539 = 8q+b with 0 ≤ b ≤ 7, and q = 67, b = 3. (ii) For N = 3i M (i ≥ 1) and n = 3k+r, replacing
n by n + N changes T3 (n) by N/3, 4N/3 or 2N/3, all ≡ 0 (mod N/3); and for fixed r the maps
k 7→ k, 4k + 2, 2k + 1 are bijections of Z/(N/3) because N/3 is odd. So n mod N 7→ T3 (n) mod N/3
is well defined and 3-to-1; iterate L times. (iii) Each branch k 7→ k, 4k + 2, 2k + 1 is an aﬀine
bijection of Z3 with unit linear coeﬀicient,
                                    P          and k 7→ 3k + r maps Z3 onto r + 3Z3 scaling Haar
measure by 31 ; hence µ(T3−1 B) = r µ(B)/3 = µ(B). By induction on j, the points whose itinerary
begins with a given word of length j form one class modulo 3j ; so the itinerary map is a continuous
bijection of compact Hausdorff spaces, hence a homeomorphism, and it conjugates T3 to the shift
by definition.

Remark 42.7 (No written link). Proposition 42.6 is a statement about T3 and the integer 539
alone. No written object (in RTA, in Parts I–III, or in any source used here) defines a map from
T3 , the carrier Ores = {21 + 9j : 0 ≤ j ≤ 538}, the packaging identities (539 = b4859/9c, 18 + 521,
3 · 68 + 5 · 67) or the linking series ΛB to gs , C, V35 or the lift representation. RTA states that
its packaging identities “are independent of any dynamical map” and of the modular status of
the series. The choice s ∈ {7, 8, 11} is attributed in RTA to the packaging arithmetic, but no
derivation of these values from it is written. The statement that the R = ST grading of the cusp

                                                  138
space is a foundation of RTA has no proof; the only proved statement of that kind is the grading
lemma of Part II’s framework (the Z/3 grading of equivariant maps by R-degree), which concerns
representations and not T3 .

42.3    Where the shadow Ξ enters
RTA describes a weight- 32 shadow Ξ = Ξeven +Ξodd as “the residual-weighted family of shifted partial
thetas of squares whose outer coeﬀicients are the residual disc-35 series”, and a weight- 21 period
candidate as its Eichler integral. Neither is written as an explicit series with stated coeﬀicients, so
no statement about Ξ can be proved from the written definitions. What is proved here bounds the
place where Ξ must enter:

 (i) at order t−1 the normalized series is governed by V35 with no correction term (Theorem 34.7);

(ii) at order t−1/2 the coeﬀicient A1 is an explicit finite sum of drift, sawtooth and ray terms
                                  √
     (Proposition 35.11) with A1 / π algebraic (Corollary 35.8); any S-covariant completion of A1
     must therefore come from a term that is not visible at order t−1 ;

(iii) at order t0 the coeﬀicient A2 is explicit (Theorem 35.6).

For the 105 companion series, Section 37 determines the shadow structure: the P -odd part has the
ξ1 -image of Theorem 37.13(i), and the P -even part has no weight-one law and the Laplacian defect
of Theorem 37.11. This does not identify Ξ, since Ξ is not written as an explicit series. At order
t−1/2 the coeﬀicient is fully explained by the completion and line terms (Theorem 38.5). Whether
a completion term of Zwegers type, with coeﬀicients derived from the error-function completion
of H7 , makes A1 or A2 S-covariant is open (Problem 1.). The numerical tests carried out so far
(Appendix D) found no closure for any regrouping of the finite sum or for the simplest sawtooth
candidate.


43 Open problems
 1. Derive the actual completion constants of H7 (erf-completion along the walls (0, 1) and
    (21, −1)), form the completed coeﬀicient A1 − Ξcompl , and decide whether it satisfies a vector-
    valued S-law of weight 12 . This would identify the shadow Ξ. The completions of the cone
    parts and their ξ1 -images are now explicit (Proposition 37.7); by Theorem 38.5, A1 minus
    these completion terms and the line terms vanishes identically, so this part of the problem is
    settled for the companion series; at order t0 the coeﬀicient minus line terms is the explicit even
    corner term of Theorem 39.4, which admits no aﬀine weight-one law (Proposition 39.5); the
    identification of Ξ itself remains open. The sawtooth candidate, which removes all W [λ], does
    not close (Appendix D).

 2. Decide whether A2 minus a completion term satisfies a vector-valued law; this is the period-
    function level. Numerically, A2 itself satisfies no pure law of weight 0, 21 or 1, and the mixed
    test with A1 , A0 is inconclusive (Appendix D).
                                   √
 3. Prove or disprove |A1 ( 12 )| = π/4 (observed numerically) and find the zero set of A1 on the
    zero set of A0 .

 4. Find a written object linking T3 , Ores or 539 to gs , C or V35 , or prove that a natural class of
    such maps does not exist.


                                                 139
 5. Extend the parity-dominance theorem of Part I beyond k = 33 and to the classes of even k
    that are not one-sign.
 6. The open problems of Part II, §32.

    • Block and tail: python3 run_pd56.py evaluates three variants; the text uses SHARP (Table
      8.2). Output pd56_results.json.
    • Reproduction: P/data/pd/regen/regen_all.sh re-ran all of the above from scratch
      in a clean directory; params56.json, cells56_sharp.json, consts56_sharp.json and
      pd56_results.json are identical to the stored files except the timing field secs of the latter
      (regen/compare.log). P/code/pd/spot_rerun.py re-runs cells 0, 100, 200, 311 and k = 3, 33
      (P/data/pd/spot_rerun_log.txt).
    • Tables 8.1–8.4 of the text are produced from the JSON files by P/code/make_tables.py
      (P/data/tables_pd.md).

    Run time on one core: cells about 20 s, run_pd56.py about 75 s for all 16 values of k and three
variants.

A.4     The modular certificate and zero sets
P/cert/modcert_copy.c (sha256 a228eaff1ac42094a28ec7b32ce495e233d3d78007d5ebd33dce4e35482caf08);
compile gcc -O2 -o modcert modcert_copy.c; P/cert/run_cert.sh runs modcert k 60000000
p for all odd k ≤ 33 and p ∈ {2305843009213693951, 4611686018427387847} (outputs
z_k<k>_p<p>.txt; log cert_jobs.log; about 3 min per job, memory below 900 MB). python3
P/code/zero_sets.py intersects the two lists, evaluates every candidate exactly, evaluates all
allowed d ≤ max Ck exactly, and writes P/data/zero_sets.json and zero_sets.md (Table 8.5,
Appendix B.1).

A.5     Source directories
The programs of A.3 and A.4 are unchanged copies of the following directories of the same repos-
itory (reports/s_family_paths/): A56_A49_audit (block, tail, cell code, modular certificate),
A58_chern_bound_audit (pair certificate, Q table), A61_clt_proofs (written proofs of the lattice-
sum, cell, and tail lemmas, PROOFS.md), A59_clt_rederivation (the independent derivation of Re-
mark 8.9), A57_gk_31_33 (the cases k = 31, 33), A46_zero_facts_audit, A47_composite_zeros,
A49_gk_runs, A50_proofs, A54_proofs2, A55_small_k_exceptions (Sections 5–7). The proofs
in Part I were written out and checked against these sources.

D     Category B record for Part III
Everything in this appendix is Category B: floating-point evaluation, least-squares fits, or numerical
ranks of real or complex matrices by singular value decomposition with relative threshold 10−9 .
None of it is used in a proof. Data, scripts and logs are in reports/bridges/BRIDGE_C_35/
and its subfolders order2, order3, order3b, order3c, order4a, and order5–order17 for items
D.10–D.14; every run was preregistered with a SHA-256 hash recorded before the run.

  D.1 Leading order on the series. t C(e(y)e−t ) was fitted at 30 rational points y using up to
      3.4 · 106 lattice points. On a first grid (t ≥ 10−3 scale) 19 points agree with I G(y) to ≤ 2%
      (most ≤ 0.1%); on t ∈ [3.1 · 10−6 , 10−4 ], 10 of the remaining 11 agree to ≤ 0.7% and y = − 12
                                                                                                    5

      differs by 6.4%. The remaining discrepancies occur at denominators ≥ 9, where the constant
      in the O(t1/2 ) term of Theorem 34.6 is large.

      and to the long cone edge Q(21, −1) = 297.5, the fitted t−1/2 coeﬀicient of q −7/20 C agrees
